clinic, worried that these recurring mood shifts were undermining their financial stability and his ability to get back into the workforce.\n- Chief Complaint: “My sleep has been all over the place—some nights I’m up planning and working on music, and other nights I can’t sleep at all or I sleep half the day.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Lately I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest, like I’ve already failed before the day even starts, and I just lie there staring at the ceiling thinking my wife would be better off without me dragging us down like this.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Most days I either forget to eat until my wife brings me a plate because I just don’t care, or I end up mindlessly inhaling chips and takeout late at night when the anxiety about money spikes, so my weight’s been bouncing around a bit.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Some nights I’m wired until four in the morning tweaking mixes and building these crazy detailed spreadsheets for new projects, and then other nights I just toss and turn thinking about the rent, or I crash so hard I sleep through half the day and miss emails and calls.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): When I’m down, even getting up to shower feels like moving through mud and my wife says I shuffle around the apartment like a zombie, but when I’m in one of those high gears I’m pacing, drumming on every surface, and hopping from my laptop to my guitar to my phone without really stopping.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): I used to be sharp with code and arranging tracks, but these past few weeks my brain feels foggy—I'll stare at a job application for an hour, reread the same paragraph over and over, and still can’t decide what to write or even which position to click on.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): There are stretches where I feel oddly amped and on edge at the same time—like I’m talking a mile a minute about all these ideas and then snapping at my wife over tiny things, like her asking about the budget, because any little question suddenly feels like an attack.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On the higher days I have this restless energy where I can’t just sit and watch a show; I’m reorganizing my hard drives, sketching out three different business plans, messaging people about collaborations, and jumping between tasks like I’m racing some four hours, wake up before my alarm buzzing with ideas, and feel totally fine, like sleep is just an inconvenience that gets in the way of me knocking out all these projects.\n- S037 invisible clock.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I get into those up phases, I catch myself thinking, ‘If I just focus for a week I can build an app, launch a YouTube channel, and fix our money the next story, barely taking a breath and not really letting her get a word in.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In the last few weeks I’ve had days where I suddenly decide I’m going to overhaul wife over tiny things, like her asking about the budget, because any little question suddenly feels like an attack.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On the higher days I have this restless energy where I can’t just sit and watch a show; I’m reorganizing my hard drives, sketching out three different business plans, messaging people about collaborations, and jumping between tasks like I’m racing some invisible clock.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I get into those up phases, I catch myself thinking, ‘If I just focus for a week I can build an app, launch a YouTube channel, and fix our money problems overnight,’ like I’ve got some special grind mode other people don’t have, even though my track record says otherwise.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During those bursts I’ll sleep three, maybe the next story, barely taking a breath and not really letting her get a word in.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In the last few weeks I’ve had days where I suddenly decide I’m going to overhaul four hours, wake up before my alarm buzzing with ideas, and feel totally fine, like sleep is just an inconvenience that gets in the way of me knocking out all these projects.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My wife keeps telling me to slow down because on those up days I’ll start talking about a new freelance plan and, before she can respond, I’ve already jumped to the next idea and the next story, barely taking a breath and not really letting her get a word in.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In the last few weeks I’ve had days where I suddenly decide I’m going to overhaul our whole life—sign up for three online courses, redesign my portfolio, reach out to everyone I’ve ever worked with—and I start all of it at once, staying busy nonstop but not actually finishing problems overnight,’ like I’ve got some special grind mode other people don’t have, even though my track record says otherwise.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During those bursts I’ll sleep three, maybe the next story, barely taking a breath and not really letting her get a word in.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In the last few weeks I’ve had days where I suddenly decide I’m going to overhaul our whole life—sign up for three online courses, redesign my portfolio, reach out to everyone I’ve ever worked with—and I start all of it at once, staying busy nonstop but not actually finishing much.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because of these ups and downs, I’ve blown past application deadlines, lost track of bills, and pulled back from friends and stuff at the community center, so it feels like I’m stuck in place while our money situation keeps getting worse.\n- duration: Looking back, I’ve been cycling through these higher-energy spurts and heavy, His wife noticed that his long‑standing pattern of cycling between periods of intense, late‑night recording and ambitious project planning, and stretches when he barely touched his guitar or coding projects, had become more pronounced as the financial pressure grew. Over the three weeks leading up to his clinic visit, he swung from drafting elaborate spreadsheets for new ventures and talking excitedly about multiple side hustles to abandoning those plans and spending days lying on the couch, increasingly worried about money yet unable to engage with job applications or community work at the center. After they fell behind on rent and he missed a deadline for a promising internship because he couldn’t bring himself to follow through, his wife urged him to see a psychiatrist at the community health tracks, but these past few weeks my brain feels foggy—I'll stare at a job application for an hour, reread the same paragraph over and over, and still can’t decide what to write or even which position wife over tiny things, like her asking about the budget, because any little question suddenly feels like an attack.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On the higher days I have this restless energy where I can’t just sit and watch a show; I’m reorganizing my hard drives, sketching out three different business plans, messaging people about collaborations, and jumping between tasks like I’m racing some invisible clock.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I get into those up phases, I catch myself thinking, ‘If I just focus for a week I can build an app, launch a YouTube channel, and fix our money problems overnight,’ like I’ve got some special grind mode other people don’t have, even though my track record says otherwise.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During those bursts I’ll sleep three, maybe four hours, wake up before my alarm buzzing with ideas, and feel totally fine, like sleep is just an inconvenience that gets in the way of me knocking out all these projects.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My wife keeps telling me to slow down because on those up days I’ll start talking about a new freelance plan and, before she can respond, I’ve already jumped to the next idea and the next story, barely taking a breath and not really letting her get a word in.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In the last few weeks I’ve had days where I suddenly decide I’m going to overhaul our whole life—sign up for three online courses, redesign my portfolio, reach out to everyone I’ve ever worked with—and I start all of it at once, staying busy nonstop but not actually finishing much.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Because of these ups and downs, I’ve blown past application deadlines, lost track of bills, and pulled back from friends and stuff at the community center, so it feels like I’m stuck in place while our money situation keeps getting worse.\n- duration: Looking back, I’ve been cycling through these higher-energy spurts and heavy, shutdown lows on and off since my early twenties, but it’s gotten more noticeable and disruptive over the past few years as I’ve tried to get a steady job.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This current low stretch has been hanging over me for around three months, ever since that long-term contract fell through and I started feeling like I couldn’t get myself moving again.\n\n[Behavioral Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: functional_impairment, Patient Background and Presenting Concern, S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity)\nAnswer strategy: Explain that while you haven't been fired from a traditional job (since you've mostly been doing freelance and part-time work), these high-energy periods definitely cause things to fall apart. Usually, though, those high-energy stretches last for a few days at a time before I start to slow down.\n\nDoctor: During these down periods, like the current three-month stretch, do you experience severe symptoms such as feelings of worthlessness, excessive guilt, or thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I end up straining my marriage because I snap at my wife over the budget and constantly talk over her. I've never really timed those periods, so I don't know exactly how many days they last when I get into that zone.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your family, friends, or doctors ever become so concerned about your safety or behavior that they felt you needed urgent professional help or evaluation, even if you weren't hospitalized?\n\nPatient: No, nobody has ever felt I was in immediate danger or needed urgent psychiatric intervention during those high times. I've blown past job application deadlines, missed a deadline for a promising internship, and we've fallen behind on rent because I lost track of bills. It's made our financial situation much worse and left me feeling completely stuck.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific time in your life when one of these high-energy periods lasted for a full week (7 days) or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure because I've never tracked the exact number of days. They definitely feel like they go on for several days at a time, but I can't say for certain if they've ever lasted a full week.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period where your behavior was so extreme or chaotic that you were completely unable to carry out any daily activities or work at all for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: No, during those high-energy times I’m actually extremely active and constantly working on projects, even if I'm jumping from task to task without finishing them. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been all over the place—some nights I’m up planning and working on music, and other nights I can’t sleep at all or I sleep half the day. During those bursts I’ll only sleep three or four hours, but I wake up buzzing with ideas and feeling totally fine, like sleep is just an inconvenience getting in the way of my projects.\n\nDoctor: Have you also experienced periods where you felt the exact opposite—feeling very down, empty, or losing all interest in your music and other activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Looking back, though, I've been cycling through these shutdown lows and high-energy spurts on and off since my early twenties.\n\nDoctor: When you have those high-energy bursts where you feel buzzed and need very little sleep, how many days in a row do those periods typically last?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure of the exact number of days because I haven't really tracked it closely. Usually, though, those high-energy stretches last for a few days at a time before I start to slow down.\n\nDoctor: During these down periods, like the current three-month stretch, do you experience severe symptoms such as feelings of worthlessness, excessive guilt, or thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I often wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest, like I’ve already failed before the day even starts. I end up straining my marriage because I snap at my wife over the budget and constantly talk over her. On top of that, I jump between unfinished projects, miss deadlines, and lose track of our bills, which has really hurt our finances.\n\nDoctor: What is the longest one of these high-energy periods has ever lasted for you—has it ever gone on for a full week or more?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure if they've ever gone on for a full week, to be honest. I've never really timed those periods, so I don't know exactly how many days they last when I get into that zone.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your family, friends, or doctors ever become so concerned about your safety or behavior that they felt you needed urgent professional help or evaluation, even if you weren't hospitalized?\n\nPatient: No, nobody has ever felt I was in immediate danger or needed urgent psychiatric intervention during those high times. It's made our financial situation much worse and left me feeling completely stuck.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific time in your life when one of these high-energy periods lasted for a full week (7 days) or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure because I've never tracked the exact number of days. They definitely feel like they go on for several days at a time, but I can't say for certain if they've ever lasted a full week.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a high-energy period where your behavior was so extreme or chaotic that you were completely unable to carry out any daily activities or work at all for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: No, during those high-energy times I’m actually extremely active and constantly working on projects, even if I'm jumping from task to task without finishing them. It's only when I crash into my low, depressed periods that I completely shut down and can't do basic daily activities or work.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your disorganization or impulsive decisions ever lead to major consequences like getting fired from a job, or did you always manage to keep things from completely falling apart?\n\nPatient: I haven't been fired from a traditional job since I mostly do freelance work, but things definitely fall apart because I start a ton of projects and business plans at once and never actually finish them. That disorganization, combined with the crash that follows, has made me blow past application deadlines and lose track of bills, which is why we've fallen behind on rent.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you ever engage in risky or impulsive behaviors, such as reckless driving, unusual spending sprees, or making major financial commitments that you couldn't afford?"

It's only when I crash into my low, depressed periods that I completely shut down and can't do basic daily activities or work.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did your disorganization or impulsive decisions ever lead to major consequences like getting fired from a job, or did you always manage to keep things from completely falling apart?\n\nPatient: I haven't been fired from a traditional job since I mostly do freelance work, but things definitely fall apart because I start a ton of projects and business plans at once and never actually finish them. That disorganization, combined with the crash that follows, has made me blow past application deadlines and lose track of bills, which is why we've fallen behind on rent.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy periods, did you ever engage in risky or impulsive behaviors, such as reckless driving, unusual spending sprees, or making major financial commitments that you couldn't afford?\n\nPatient: I don't recall doing anything really reckless like speeding or going on big spending sprees during those high times. I do tend to get overly ambitious, though, and end up taking on way too many projects at once thinking I can fix our money problems overnight."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
